Let me create a summary of what I've learned and then start implementing the UnifiedSymbolResolver.\n\nKey patterns I've identified:\n\n1. **Scope Chain Walking** (in `LookupSymbolsInternal`):\n   - Walk through a chain of binders (scopes)\n   - Call `LookupSymbolsInSingleBinder` on each scope\n   - Stop when a viable result is found\n\n2. **Single Binder Lookup** (virtual method):\n   - Each binder type overrides `LookupSymbolsInSingleBinder` to look in its specific scope\n   - Can look in local variables, type parameters, members, etc.\n\n3. **Member Lookup** (in Debug.Assert(options.AreValid());\n\n            var result = new SymbolResolutionResult();\n            var lookupResult = LookupResult.GetInstance();\n\n            try\n            {\n                       }\n                    }\n                    finally\n                    {\n                        scopeResult.Free();\n                    }\n                }\n\n                // Populate the result with final symbols and diagnostics\n                result.SetLookupResult(lookupResult);\n            }\n            finally\n            {\n                lookupResult.Free();\n            }\n\n            return result;\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// <summary>\n    /// Strategy interface for symbol resolution in different contexts.\n    /// It refactors the separate code paths for expression binding, type resolution, and member access into reusable components.\n\n## Files Created\n\n### 1. UnifiedSymbolResolver.cs\nThe main implementation file containing:\n\n#### Core Classes:\n- **`UnifiedSymbolResolver`** (sealed, internal)\n  - Main orchestrator for symbol resolution using a strategy pattern\n  - `Resolve()` method: Walks scope chain and delegates to strategy\n  - Takes a `Binder` and `ISymbolResolutionStrategy` in constructor\n\n#### Strategy Interface:\n- **`ISymbolResolutionStrategy`** (interface, internal)\n  - `ResolveSingleScope()`: Resolves symbols in a single scope\n  - `ShouldTerminateSearch()`: Determines when to stop searching up the scope chain\n\n#### Concrete Strategy Implementations:\n\n1. **`TypeResolutionStrategy`** (sealed, internal)\n   - Used for type resolution in base classes and constraint clauses\n   - Delegates to `LookupSymbolsInSingleBinder`\n   - Terminates when viable result is found\n\n2. **`MemberAccessStrategy`** (sealed, internal)\n   - Used for member access (method calls, property access)\n   - Delegates to `LookupMembersInternal` with a qualifier type\n   - Terminates on viable members\n\n3. **`NamespaceResolutionStrategy`** (sealed, internal)\n   - Used for namespace-qualified names and imports\n   - Delegates to `LookupSymbolsInSingleBinder`\n   - Terminates on viable results\n\n#### Result Type:\n- **`SymbolResolutionResult`** (sealed, internal)\n  - Encapsulates resolution results\n  - Properties: `Symbols`, `Kind`, `Error`, `IsComplete`, `IsViable`, `HasSymbols`\n  - Methods: `SetLookupResult()`, `MergeResults()`, `TerminateSearch()`\n  - Exposes `LookupResult` for interoperability with existing Roslyn code\n\n### 2. Nested type access\n- `TestUsingDirectiveResolution`: Using directives\n- `TestAliasResolution`: Type aliases\n- `TestInterfaceImplementationMembers`: Interface members\n- `TestTypeParameterConstraintResolution`: Constrained type parameters\n- `TestExtensionMethodResolution`: Extension method lookup\n- `TestSymbolHiding`: Method hiding\n- `TestNamespaceQualifiedLookup`: Qualified name resolution\n- `TestStaticMemberAccess`: Static member access\n- `TestLambdaParameterResolution`: Lambda parameter scoping\n\n#### Error Handling Tests:\n- `TestInaccessibleMemberError`: Inaccessible member errors\n- `TestUndefinedSymbolError`: Undefined symbol errors\n- `TestAmbiguousSymbolError`: Ambiguous symbol handling\n\n## Architecture Highlights\n\n### Strategy Pattern Benefits:\n1. **Separation of Concerns**: Resolution strategies encapsulate context-specific logic\n2. **Reusability**: Same scope-walking logic reused across different contexts\n3. **Extensibility**: New strategies can be added without modifying core logic\n4. **Testability**: Each strategy can be tested independently\n\n### Scope Chain Walking:\nThe `Resolve()` method implements the core pattern:\n- Iterates through scope chain (linked list of binders)\n- Delegates to strategy for each scope\n- Merges results using `LookupResult.MergeEqual()`\n- Respects strategy's termination conditions\n- Properly manages pool-allocated `LookupResult` objects\n\n### Common Patterns Consolidated:\nFrom `Binder_Lookup.cs`:\n- `LookupSymbolsInternal`: Scope chain iteration\n- `LookupSymbolsInSingleBinder`: Per-scope resolution (virtual method)\n- `LookupMembersInternal`: Member lookup with qualification\n- `LookupResult` merging logic\n\n## Integration Points\n\nThe implementation integrates with existing Roslyn types:\n- `Binder`: Base class with `LookupSymbolsInSingleBinder()` and `LookupMembersInternal()`\n- `LookupResult`: Pool-allocated result container\n- `LookupResultKind`: Result classification (Viable, Inaccessible, NonViable, Empty)\n- `DiagnosticInfo`: Error information\n- `CompoundUseSiteInfo<AssemblySymbol>`: Use-site diagnostics\n- `ArrayBuilder<Symbol>`: Dynamic symbol collection\n- `ConsList<TypeSymbol>`: Circular reference tracking\n\n## Design Decisions\n\n1. **Strategy Interface Design**:\n   - `ResolveSingleScope()` receives all necessary parameters\n   - `ShouldTerminateSearch()` kept simple for clarity\n   - Both methods are stateless to avoid mutable state issues\n\n2. **Result Wrapping**:\n   - `SymbolResolutionResult` wraps `LookupResult` for convenience\n   - Exposes underlying `LookupResult` via property for backward compatibility\n   - Maintains existing pool management patterns\n\n3. **Scope Termination**:\n   - Each strategy controls when search terminates\n   - Allows different behaviors (e.g., type vs. member resolution)\n   - Respects label search boundaries via options\n\n4. **Error Handling**:\n   - Preserves original diagnostic information\n   - Supports both viable and non-viable results\n   - Maintains existing error classification scheme\n\n## Success Criteria
